# Autopilot decision log

One entry for every decision a session takes that the owner used to take, newest last. Rules: `docs/specs/2026-10-06-autopilot-design.md`. Each entry: date; what was decided; the rule it rests on; the option not taken; the commit; for a commit, the review rounds it took and the must-fix findings of each, in the reviewer's words.

## 2026-10-06

1. **Step 1 of the autopilot design is built in the session that proposed it, not in a new one.** Rule: change 1 (work inside the repository proceeds by the best recommendation). Not taken: a new session, which the proposal had suggested; the session's context stood at 57% against a stop at 80%, and the owner's word was to go on. Commit: the one that adds this file.
2. **Changes 4, 5 and 6 (a merge by rule; a scheduled task; the decision lines applied to the `plan-01` run of 2026-10-06) are held until the owner confirms them in one line.** Rule: the heavy-step reviewer's findings that the approval of the first two is inferred from the owner's usual sentence of delegation and both are hard to take back, and that the third chooses a rule for a run whose result is known. Not taken: treating the sentence as covering them, as the proposal's own stated reading did.
3. **P5b's way on waits with change 6.** Once confirmed it is taken from the decision lines, not asked again: one second freeze of the `bk-plan` text is allowed, then a run identical in registration to the first, only if every edit can be a general rule; otherwise P5b closes unmerged (the pilot, step 4). Until then the owner's choice among (a), (b) and (c) of the handoff `2026-10-06-p5b-run-result.md` stands open. Step 2 needs no confirmation and comes first; step 3 needs change 5.

**Requests waiting for the owner** (gathered here, as change 1 says): one line confirming changes 4 and 6, or either. Change 5 is not asked yet: the three things named in the spec's "What this changes" are settled through the gate first.

### Review record of the commit that adds this file

- **Round 1** (two reviewers in parallel, read-only). Sonnet, on facts and consistency, four must-fix: the count of questions to the owner (six in the records, not eight; three review stops, not four, and not one cause); the decision lines applied to a run registered before them without saying so, and nothing keeping the second run's bars; the handoff's resume prompt still telling a cold start to wait; three reservations of the old rules missing from the owner's list (installing software, cloud sessions, the guard). Opus, adversarial, nine must-fix: a session could widen its own leave and nothing said who writes `RUN`; a second run could move the task, rubric or readers instead of a bar; a merge by rule after a tuned second run; the approval of the two hard-to-reverse points inferred; the approval of bars and the driver lock dropped without being listed; the memory path contradicting "nothing else under `~/.claude`"; scheduled tasks unbounded; the `AGENTS.md` line binding hand-driven sessions and overriding COUNCIL for design changes; the incident rule letting the session pick the favourable handling. All worked in, with the reviewers' own wording where they gave it; the rewrite is new text, so it went to round 2. (These two entries summarise: the reviewers' replies in full are in the session's transcript, not in the repository.)
- **Round 2** (the same two roles, fresh agents). Sonnet, four must-fix: one decision line named the merge by rule without saying it waits; the `AGENTS.md` line did not say changes 4 and 5 are not in force; `state.md` held `RUN` written by a session while the text said a session never writes it; the state after a reviewer's "no" to a second freeze was undefined. Opus, nine must-fix: the gate still let a session change this file through the advisor; bars self-approved (nobody asked whether a bar is lenient); who records the owner's confirmation; the decision lines applied to a run with a known result without the owner having said so; "without asking" against an instruction the owner gives in a session; an edit re-worded and re-asked until yes; who lists the candidate handlings of an incident; no counters for sessions, audits and rounds; the lock deadlocking after a crash without saying so. All worked in with the reviewers' wording; round 3 followed.
- **Round 3** (the same two roles, fresh agents). Sonnet, two must-fix: "a session only ever writes `PAUSE`" beside "the first `RUN` is written by the session", unexplained in `state.md` and `AGENTS.md`; the handoff's added sentence too broad (it kept "no memory write without a yes", which change 3 replaces). Opus, "fit to commit: no", six must-fix: nothing reserved P5b's (a)/(b)/(c) choice for the owner while change 6 waits; the stop conditions unordered, so a second session could commit `PAUSE` into a live checkout; the first `RUN` readable as a reusable licence, and a missing switch undefined; "may only tighten" judged by the author; a partial confirmation undefined and no criterion for an owner-driven session; no rule where no owner-approved registration of a kind exists. All worked in with the reviewers' wording; round 4 followed.
- **Round 4** (the same two roles, fresh agents). Sonnet, one must-fix: the stop conditions said "the lock first" while the list put the unclean tree first. Opus, "fit to commit: no, two small text fixes, then yes": a `PAUSE` could be committed on a branch where the switch is not read; "freezes, registers or runs" left closing P5b and opening P5c open to a session. All three worked in with the reviewers' exact wording; round 5, the last the gate allows, followed. Noted by Opus for the day changes 4, 5 or 6 are confirmed, not fixed now: an owner-typed message cannot be told from one another agent sends into a scheduled session; which sessions count for the session and audit counters when unattended; a `PAUSE` after a fifth failed round leaves the failed change uncommitted in the tree. After round 4 the main session added one paragraph of its own to the spec ("Three things a reviewer named are settled…"), which went to round 5 as new text.
- **Round 5** (the same two roles, fresh agents). Sonnet: no must-fix. Opus, "fit to commit: no (one one-line fix, then yes)", one must-fix, caused by that added paragraph: it said the three things are settled before the owner is asked to confirm change 5, while "Requests waiting for the owner" in this log already asked for 4, 5 and 6. Corrected with the reviewer's exact replacement (the request now names changes 4 and 6 only). **A fifth round with a must-fix: by the gate the session stopped, committed nothing and reported to the owner**, with the recommendation of one more round on the corrected line only, then the suite and the commit. The owner wrote, verbatim: "Audit kỹ các xử lý ở trên, tiếp tục theo khuyến nghị." An instruction the owner gives in a session wins for that session, so a sixth round ran, on the corrected line and this record. The same message is **not** taken as the confirmation of changes 4 and 6, which the spec asks to be a typed line naming the changes: both stay unconfirmed.
- **Round 6** (by the owner's word above): see the commit message; no must-fix, or the commit was not made.

4. **The commit of the rules was made by the owner's own hand** (`5fb65f7`, 2026-10-06). The session's commit command was refused by the app's permission classifier as a self-modification (the change touches `AGENTS.md`, the rules that govern the session); the session did not look for a way round it, reported, and gave the owner the three commands. The owner ran them and said so ("3 lệnh trên tôi đã chạy rồi"); the session checked: six files, pushed, tree clean, the switch at `RUN`. Consequence, for the record: on this machine a session cannot by itself commit a change to `AGENTS.md`; the owner does, which fits "What stays the owner's".
5. **Round 6 left three points it did not call must-fix; they are open**: this log's round 5 entry does not say that no `PAUSE` was written (the switch was not on `main` yet); the round 4 entry ties the three things still to settle to changes 4, 5 and 6 where the spec ties them to change 5; the spec speaks of one line of confirmation where there will be at least two (4 and 6 first, 5 later). To be settled with the three things of change 5, through the gate.
6. **Step 2 (the keep-alive check) is not run in this session.** Rule: change 1. Not taken: running it now. A check of sleep means something only over a stretch longer than the machine's idle timeout with nobody at the keyboard, and the owner was present; it is the first thing of the next session, started and then left alone.

7. **The session's audit of itself, and what follows from it.** At the owner's request the session audited the whole session and put eight recommendations; the owner replied, verbatim: "Audit kỹ các xử lý ở trên, tiếp tục theo khuyến nghị." Rule: change 1. Taken as the working plan of the next sessions, each still through the gate where it changes a file:
   - **Sessions stop by cost**: one phase a session, and a stop at about 300,000 tokens of context, read with `get_usage`. This only stops earlier than the owner's rule of 80% of the window, which stays the upper bound. (The session that wrote this stood at 652,113 tokens, 65% of its window, at its last `get_usage` reading before this entry; every late turn re-read all of it.)
   - **The next registration of a measurement puts its sessions where a bar is**: for a text against its own earlier version, twelve sessions on each kit branch and eight on the sources, no separate no-plugin branch in the run when the floor is known from the calibration. It is a recommendation for registrations to come, each of which still passes the heavy-step review; it does not apply to a second run, which keeps the first run's registration.
   - **One fact in one place**: figures live in the spec; a handoff and `docs/status.md` point to them instead of repeating them.
   - **The gate by stakes, as the spec already says**: one fresh reviewer for every commit, including a commit of handoff, status or log only; the adversarial second reviewer for the registration of a measurement, the record of its result and a merge into `main`; the advisor for a change to the spec outside the owner-held sections.
   - **Change 5 (a scheduled task) is not pursued for now**: not asked of the owner until the pilot has run well over several sessions.
   - **The advisor reads the autopilot design once, in the next session**, where the context is short.
   - **A shorter Autopilot line for `AGENTS.md`**: the session drafts it, the owner commits it.
   - Not taken: loosening a bar so that the `bk-plan` text passes; closing P5b now; switching on the merge by rule and the scheduled task before any pilot; more review rounds or more models for every commit.
   - **Not covered by that reply**: change 6. It asks for a typed line naming it, and the reply does not; P5b stays where it is.

11. **`PAUSE`: five review rounds on the rewrite of the resume prompt ended with a must-fix (2026-10-07, session 3, the stretch after a `/clear`, driven by the owner).** The owner's words, verbatim: "Kiểm tra prompt đã thực sự chuẩn, chính xác chưa, tốt nhất có thể và phù hợp nhất có thể chưa?", "Trong trường hợp cần thiết, chạy loop để kiểm tra, tối ưu cho tôi." and "Sau cùng, xử lý theo các khuyến nghị đã đưa ra." The recommendations were: rewrite the resume prompt of `second-freeze.md` and test the hand-over once (one `CronCreate` wake-up, then `clear_session`, the last two calls of the turn; entry 10(c) planned the test; change 5 is not used). The prompt was checked against the repository and rewritten into a new handoff, `docs/handoff/2026-10-07-clear-chain.md`, with one fresh Sonnet reviewer per round (cold-start drill and fact check, read-only). Must-fix by round, in the reviewers' words condensed: round 1: the test could record "survived" when no clear happened, and a "pasted by hand means it did not survive" rule stuck to a fixed file; the chain had no stop condition; the author had added a clause with no source ("only when the owner has said to run the measurement"; dropped). Round 2: the order of step 5 contradicted itself; a parent commit was named wrong (`7896625`; the parent of `afa1a46` is `2cdf4d5`). Round 3: the schedule-and-clear step had no preconditions and a fixed wake-up time could already be past. Round 4: the entry for this decision pointed at a round the handoff did not hold; the clear check of a session that had waited counted the owner's paste and the "wait" reply as earlier turns, so a later wake-up would have been recorded backwards. Round 5: the same repair was half done: the waiting session must call tools (`git log`, read the handoff, read the clock) before it can answer "wait", and the clear check counts tool calls; the wake-up that reaches that session would still read "the clear did not happen". **Rule: the gate, "at most five rounds for one change; a fifth round with a must-fix sets the switch to `PAUSE`"; so `state.md` is set to `PAUSE` by this session, which writes nothing else.** Nothing of the rewrite is committed (the gate forbids "a note that part of it was not read"): it stays in the working tree, uncommitted: the new `docs/handoff/2026-10-07-clear-chain.md`, one pointer line each at the top of `2026-10-07-pilot-start.md` and `2026-10-07-second-freeze.md`, and one line of `docs/status.md`. What happens to an uncommitted change when a fifth round sets `PAUSE` is one of the three things the spec (line 69) leaves open; this session left it in the tree and did not stash, commit or discard it. The hand-over test was not run, and no wake-up was scheduled. The suite on the tree with those edits: 206 of 206 (checked three times). **The one repair round 5 asks for** (its words, condensed): the clear check in section 0 of the prompt must also leave out the read-only tool calls of the "wait" branch (`git log`, reading the handoff, reading the clock). Lesser findings of round 5, also open: in step 5(iv) the lock is already released at (iii), so "hold the lock until after this small commit" should read "take the lock again before the small commit, release it and check with `ls` after"; a wake-up that is delayed by a slow approval of `clear_session` can arrive after "five minutes", and a second line "the clear did not happen" can follow a line "did not survive", so say which line wins; a wake-up that exits because of the lock should print its clear-check result to the owner instead of only saying "survived". The owner decides: lift the stop for one more round of the fix (an instruction of the owner in a session wins for that session, spec), or leave it; `RUN` is written only by the owner. Not taken: committing the prompt with a note; stashing or discarding the work; more rounds without the owner's word. **Session line, 2026-10-07, session 3 (the stretch after the `/clear`, opened by the owner):** commits that passed the gate: none in this stretch other than the one that adds this line (a `PAUSE` write, outside the gate); sessions since the last audit: 3 (this stretch counted as a session of its own); no audit has run yet; the audit session is due after the fourth.

14. **`PAUSE`: the second run of `plan-01` ran on another model than the first, the registration has no rule for it, and a candidate handling changes a verdict (2026-10-07, session 4).** Rule: "Decisions fixed before the data", "An incident a registration has no rule for ...: if every registered bar's verdict is the same under every candidate handling, the session takes the handling least favourable to the kit and logs all of them with their figures; otherwise `PAUSE`. The candidates are listed by the heavy-step reviewer, given only the registration and the incident; any handling it names that changes a verdict: `PAUSE`." **So `state.md` is set to `PAUSE` by this commit, which holds the switch and this entry and nothing else.** What happened before it, each step through the gate, all on `p5b-bk-plan`: the dry run, the reach probe (passed, 2 of 2) and the S trial (accepted), `00a5276` (one round, no must-fix); the four rounds, 32 sessions, every call `exit=0`, no session cut, no sleep event inside a round, the sixteen counted directories written before any plan was read, `3f77ff1` (one round, no must-fix); the gate of the day passed (each reader 70 of 70), 32 plans read in three batches by fresh pairs, no reply refused; the tally. **The incident**: the tally prints `claude-sonnet-5-5` for every one of the 32 sessions; the first run's sessions were `claude-sonnet-5`; the registered wording is "model alias `sonnet`", and the rule of a second run asks for a registration "identical to the first in ... model". The alias resolved to another model on the day; nothing in the repository checks the resolved model. Found when the tally was read, after every session and reading. The record, with the figures and no verdict, and the run's evidence: `2ce999d` on `p5b-bk-plan` (two rounds, a fact reviewer and an adversarial reviewer each, Sonnet; round 1 must-fix, in their words condensed: "its reply is in the decision log of `main`" was "false today"; the section "presents the primary (item 1) and never says" that the reporting line on P2 or P5 is not applicable to P5; round 2 both "MUST-FIX: none"). That record was committed before this `PAUSE` because the evidence lay only in the session's temporary directory; it writes no verdict and decides nothing that is the owner's.
   - **The heavy-step reviewer's reply on the incident, in its own words** (a fresh Sonnet agent, read-only, given the registration and the incident and nothing of the results): "1. Does the registration decide this case? No. It names the model only as "model alias `sonnet` (the three floor sessions' `init` events name `claude-sonnet-5`, as `plan-tally.cjs` prints from their streams)" (Step 5, "Design of the run"). The second run repeats it as "model alias `sonnet`". The parenthetical describes what the alias resolved to in the calibration. It is not a rule. Nothing in the spec says what happens if the resolved ID differs. The driver does not check it, and `plan-run.cjs` has no "model" match. `plan-tally.cjs` only prints the models it finds per branch. The nearest text is the label rule: "Never a statement beyond this task and model." The standing rules say the second run's registration is "identical to the first in ... model", and that changing "the model ... after its data is known" is never done by rule. The first sentence is arguably unmet. The second is the reason nothing can be re-pinned now. The incident therefore falls under "An incident a registration has no rule for". 2. Candidate handlings (each compared with "counts as registered"). A. Counts as registered. The alias is what was registered, so bars 1 to 6 and the statement against the sources are read as written. This is the baseline. B. Counts, but labelled "on claude-sonnet-5-5, this task" and not pooled with or compared to run 1. The bars are read the same way. CANNOT CHANGE A VERDICT: only the wording around the verdicts changes. C. Not a valid second run: the registration is not "identical in model", so the run is void. No bar has a verdict, and there is no "better than its sources" or "no clear difference" statement. CAN CHANGE A VERDICT: if the bars would all hold or the sources test would pass, void removes that result. If the bars would be missed, the text stays on its branch either way, because a rerun would be a third run. D. The within-run tests count (bars 1, 2, and the test against the sources); the absolute-count bars (3, 4, 5) and G1/G2 are not judged. All four branches ran the same model, interleaved, so a within-run test stays fair. The bars with absolute thresholds depend on the model's level. CAN CHANGE A VERDICT: a held or missed bar 3, 4 or 5 becomes "not judged". E. The run is exploratory and unregistered, and the first run's result stands (missed). CAN CHANGE A VERDICT: any possible pass is discarded. If the bars would be missed, the result is the same as A. F. The run counts, but its pass cannot be reported as replicating run 1. This is about wording, not verdicts. CANNOT CHANGE A VERDICT. 3. Least favourable to the kit. C, void. It never gives the kit more than A: if the bars would hold there is no pass, and if they would be missed the closure is the same. E comes second. Because C and D can change a verdict, the standing rule gives PAUSE, not "take the least favourable handling". 4. False or unsupported under every handling: saying the second run has "the same model" as the first, or that its registration was identical in model; saying the run replicates or confirms the first run, or pooling the two runs' figures (run 1 was on `claude-sonnet-5` and run 2 on `claude-sonnet-5-5`, so no cross-run comparison is clean); attributing any between-run change, including K-after's P5, P1 or G2 moving, to the second-freeze edits (the model changed in the same step, so the change is confounded; this also weakens the registered line that "a P5 gain comes from edit 2"); saying the model change had no effect (neither model was run on both texts, so the effect is unmeasured); saying why the alias resolved differently ("no file of the repository changed it" is a negative only); any statement about "Sonnet" in general (at most it can be about `claude-sonnet-5-5` on `plan-01`); anything about the readers' resolved model (reader A is also the alias `sonnet`, and the incident records only the sessions' `init` events, so the readers' model, and whether it matches run 1's readers, is unverified)." (Line breaks and list marks of the reply are run together here; no word is changed.)

Under A, B and F the figures of bars 1 to 5 are at or past their registered numbers; under C and E the second run gives no result and the first run's (bars 1, 2 and 4 missed) is the only one; under D bars 1 and 2 and the test against the sources are past their numbers and bars 3 to 5 are not judged. **Under every handling nothing merges by rule** (a text that passes only on its second run is the owner's to merge; the guard runs only on the owner's word), **and no third run is made** ("At most two runs of one skill's text on one task, ever").
